# Notable games and the games industry

See [purpose](purpose.md) for the topic charter (in-scope / out-of-scope) and
`CLAUDE.md` "Cross-cutting relevance criteria" for the meta-bar.

## Summary

Games-of-note in 2026 is defined by a single defining paradox: **record top-line revenue running alongside historic developer contraction**. The global games market cleared **$200B for the first time ever in 2025, closing at $201.6B (+9.1% YoY, Newzoo)** — PC was the standout segment at +12% ($43.6B, the strongest annual growth in Newzoo's historical dataset), mobile stayed largest at $113.3B, and console grew just 2.8% ($44.7B) [[global-games-revenue-breached-200b-in-2025-newzoo-bbccd35d]]. Yet the same window has produced the most severe wave of studio closures and layoffs the industry has tracked. Around that paradox sit consolidation among the major publishers, a bifurcating live-service market, an unusually strong release slate fronted by GTA 6, a **platform-holder-driven exit from physical media**, and sharpening labor/genAI/regulation fault lines. The full dated chronology lives in `## Recent updates` below; this is the current-state synthesis.

> The global games market passed $200 billion in revenue for the first time in 2025, closing at $201.6 billion according to Newzoo's latest quarterly update to the Global Games Market Report.

**Consolidation & contraction.** The major-publisher map is being redrawn: EA agreed to a record **$55B Saudi-PIF-led take-private**, the largest leveraged buyout ever [[ea-sets-a-new-8-billion-sales-record-just-months-after-laying-off-battlefield-6-developers-9ddae332]]; Ubisoft split its franchises into Tencent-backed Vantage Studios [[ubisoft-and-tencent-launch-vantage-studios-a43d07e1]]; Paramount Skydance is acquiring Warner Bros. Discovery [[paramount-acknowledged-the-existence-of-warner-bros-games-faa1d7b1]] and stood up a unified **Paramount Game Studios** even before the WB close [[paramount-unveils-new-unified-paramount-games-studio-3b154906]]; and Embracer is spinning off **Fellowship Entertainment** (Tomb Raider, Lord of the Rings, 11 studios) [[embracer-to-spin-off-major-video-game-franchises-and-studios-aa29c338]]. Ubisoft is structurally winding down its two-decade multi-studio model — closing Winnipeg and Belgrade for up to 380 cuts and refocusing Barcelona on Rainbow Six [[ubisoft-cutting-jobs-and-shutting-winnipeg-and-belgrade-studios-691886c0]]. Closures and layoffs span every tier — Sony shut Bluepoint and Dark Outlaw Games [[playstation-boss-blames-bluepoint-cuts-on-budgets-and-players-f38a5da9]] [[playstation-shuts-down-call-of-duty-leads-second-studio-4b2ca078]], EA cut Battlefield staff months after a record launch [[ea-has-laid-off-an-undisclosed-number-of-battlefield-developers-55eb4720]], Nacon's insolvency liquidated Spiders [[greedfall-developer-spiders-has-been-liquidated-c72bc9fe]], and Behaviour Interactive cut 95 more jobs in a second wave within two months [[behaviour-interactive-cuts-95-jobs-in-strategic-corporate-change-c6d65431]].

> Electronic Arts announced that it's sold itself in a $55 billion deal to an investor consortium made up of Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund, Jared Kushner's Affinity Partners, and the private equity firm Silver Lake. The $55 billion take-private deal is the largest leveraged buyout (LBO) of all time.

> EA has laid off an undisclosed number of Battlefield developers just months after the record-breaking launch of Battlefield 6. There have been layoffs across all four studios within the division, which comprises internal studios Criterion, DICE, Ripple Effect, and Motive.

**Xbox's "reset" is now the largest single layoff event in gaming history.** Microsoft's gaming division is in the most severe first-party retrenchment of the year, and as of **July 6, 2026** it is confirmed rather than rumored. New Xbox CEO Asha Sharma announced what she called the **"most significant restructure" in the division's history**: **1,600 roles cut immediately and up to another 1,600 across the fiscal year ending June 30, 2027 — up to 3,200 total, roughly 20% of the division** [[xbox-to-lay-off-up-to-3-200-staffers-in-most-significant-restructure-in-company-history-257e9572]] [[xbox-to-cut-20-of-workforce-plans-to-divest-of-five-studios-df966a4a]]. The cuts hit **almost every division — Activision, Bethesda/ZeniMax, Blizzard, King, Mojang, and Xbox Game Studios** — and Duke Nukem creator George Broussard called it **"the largest single layoff event in gaming history"** [[largest-lay-off-event-in-gaming-duke-nukem-creator-shares-insight-into-xbox-demise-9abad5b9]]. Sharma framed the reset around a business she called **"not healthy,"** running at **margins three to ten times lower than comparable businesses** [[xbox-to-cut-20-of-workforce-plans-to-divest-of-five-studios-df966a4a]]. It follows June 15's leadership shake-up — **Craig Duncan stepped down as head of Xbox Game Studios** (chief of staff Louise O'Connor also leaving Microsoft), the studios now reporting to chief content officer Matt Booty pending a replacement [[xbox-game-studios-boss-resigns-ahead-of-mass-layoffs-368649ed]]. Studio-level cuts are now quantified: **id Software (Doom) lost ~95 employees — roughly half the studio — and Bethesda Game Studios 35** [[xbox-layoffs-hit-id-software-and-zenimax-as-focus-shifts-to-bigger-franchises-01022887]]; **ZeniMax Online Studios (Elder Scrolls Online) was "seemingly gutted,"** with up to half the team impacted [[the-elder-scrolls-online-team-seemingly-gutted-by-xbox-layoffs-83154866]]; and **Obsidian (Avowed, Pentiment) lost ~60–70 roles, about a quarter of its staff** [[report-obsidian-losing-around-1-4-of-its-staff-in-xbox-reset-4ed90369]]. A July 19 Maryland WARN Act notice now quantifies the ZeniMax scope and leadership vacuum: **213 from ZeniMax Online + 166 from ZeniMax Media = 379 total** (effective September 4, 2026), and ZeniMax Online's leadership was **stripped wholesale — studio head Joe Burba (14-year veteran), ESO executive producer Susan Kath, production director Ala Diaz, and game director Rich Lambert all laid off — with ESO having lost at least 60% of its staff over the past year** [[zenimax-online-studios-leadership-is-part-of-the-379-laid-off-workers-9588dc4d]]. The reset's starkest human juxtaposition: **DOOM: The Dark Ages — Revelations launched July 7 to positive reviews the day after Xbox cut roughly half of id Software** [[xbox-layoffs-cut-id-software-in-half-as-doom-the-dark-ages-dlc-launches-01f6ebe4]].

> Xbox is set to lay off 1,600 staffers now and up to another 1,600 over the next year in what new CEO Asha Sharma calls the 'most significant restructure' in the Microsoft division's history.

> Xbox CEO Asha Sharma stated that the business today is 'not healthy' and that Xbox is operating at margins three to 10 times lower than comparable businesses.

> Duke Nukem creator George Broussard claimed that Xbox's layoffs will be 'the largest single layoff event in gaming history.'

> Craig Duncan is stepping down as head of Xbox Game Studios. Louise O'Connor, Chief of Staff at Xbox Game Studios, will also exit Microsoft. The studios that reported to Duncan will report to Xbox Chief Content Officer Matt Booty until a replacement is found.

> ZeniMax layoffs confirmed: id Software losing 95 employees and Bethesda 35, as Microsoft shifts focus to bigger franchises.

> The Elder Scrolls Online team is seemingly gutted by Xbox layoffs, with the team possibly up to half impacted.

> Obsidian Entertainment is losing around a quarter of its staff in the Xbox Reset, with approximately 60–70 roles affected.

> The cuts include Joe Burba, a 14-year veteran of the studio who was appointed to run it last July, as well as The Elder Scrolls Online executive producer Susan Kath, production director Ala Diaz, and studio game director Rich Lambert. Over half of the layoffs come from the Elder Scrolls Online studio, which has lost at least 60 percent of its staff in the past year.

> DOOM: The Dark Ages Revelations, the first major DLC for the bombastic shooter, launched on July 7, 2026 across Xbox Series consoles, PC, and PS5. On July 6th, Xbox laid off roughly half of id Software, a day before the expansion launched.

**The five-studio divestiture terms are now concrete — and milder than "closure" for most.** The five studios cut loose from Xbox Game Studios resolve as follows. **Double Fine (Psychonauts) and Compulsion Games (Peabody-winning *South of Midnight*) transition back to independence, retaining rights to ALL of their IP — including work created after Microsoft acquired them — with Microsoft providing runway funding** to start new games and find future investors/publishers [[xbox-spins-off-compulsion-games-double-fine-productions-undead-labs-and-ninja-theory-while-blade-maker-arkane-reviews-potential-strategic-options-a6f44600]]. **Ninja Theory (Hellblade) and Undead Labs (State of Decay) have signed contracts to "join new ownership,"** receiving funding to finish **Senua** and **State of Decay 3**; buyer identities are undisclosed but **set to be revealed later this year**, with Ninja Theory's new owner reported as a done deal [[xbox-spins-off-compulsion-games-double-fine-productions-undead-labs-and-ninja-theory-while-blade-maker-arkane-reviews-potential-strategic-options-a6f44600]] [[xbox-will-lay-off-3200-workers-and-cut-four-studios-loose-20e5ef59]]. This spin-off/sale path is **structurally novel for Microsoft**, whose game-studio acquisitions since 2018 had been absorptions, not partial divestitures; Ninja Theory had already **cancelled Project Mara** to consolidate all 85 staff onto Senua [[ninja-theory-cancels-ambitious-horror-title-to-focus-on-senua-d4f98883]]. The fifth studio, **Arkane Lyon** (Marvel's Blade), has entered **"required consultation with its Works Council to review potential strategic options"** — a French legal process that "could take months" [[xbox-spins-off-compulsion-games-double-fine-productions-undead-labs-and-ninja-theory-while-blade-maker-arkane-reviews-potential-strategic-options-a6f44600]]. The earlier reporting that Microsoft would **cancel Marvel's Blade outright is now superseded**: Blade is delayed to a **late-2027** window (a slip, over budget) but per Sharma is **not among the announced games being cancelled**, and Microsoft states **"none of our first-party publicly announced games or projects are being cancelled"** [[no-announced-xbox-games-are-getting-axed-in-microsoft-layoffs-company-says-bde5adba]]. That official line is now in tension with the **first *named* project cancellation tied to the Reset**: Bloomberg's Jason Schreier reported (July 8) that **Obsidian cancelled a planned Avowed sequel** — on track to be announced within a year — plus other projects, and has **pivoted to a new Fallout game led by *Fallout: New Vegas* director Josh Sawyer, with Bethesda reportedly co-developing** [[report-obsidian-cancels-avowed-sequel-to-begin-work-on-a-new-fallout-game-2d7ec4f3]]. Microsoft's statement stays technically consistent — Avowed 2 was never *publicly* announced — but it is a concrete cancellation nonetheless, alongside a California WARN notice confirming **52 Obsidian layoffs** [[report-obsidian-cancels-avowed-sequel-to-begin-work-on-a-new-fallout-game-2d7ec4f3]]. The reset also reached **external third-party partners**: Microsoft ended its publishing deal on IO Interactive's online RPG **Project Fantasy**, forcing layoffs at the Hitman studio **despite 007 First Light selling 3M copies in two weeks** — IO will seek other funding or self-publish [[io-interactive-facing-layoffs-as-it-cuts-ties-with-project-fantasy-partner-believed-to-be-xbox-830869aa]] [[io-interactive-fantasy-ip-loses-external-partner-amid-xbox-cuts-e27f40c3]]. Counter-currents persist: Kojima Productions' **OD** is reportedly safe as an external partner rather than an owned studio [[hideo-kojima-s-od-is-reportedly-safe-from-upcoming-xbox-cuts-4a4b1eeb]], and a deliberate return-to-exclusivity posture (Gears of War: E-Day, Clockwork Revolution) sits alongside continued multiplatform releases like Halo: Campaign Evolved on PS5 [[xbox-console-exclusives-are-returning-on-a-case-by-case-basis-e3e71255]].

> Ninja Theory and Undead Labs have signed contracts to 'join new ownership,' and both studios will receive funding to complete and 'grow' their respective in-development games, Senua and State of Decay 3. Microsoft isn't revealing what companies have purchased the studios yet.

> Four Xbox studios — Ninja Theory, Undead Labs, Compulsion Games and Double Fine Productions, which collectively employ approximately 350 people — will be spun out or sold. Ninja Theory and Undead Labs are set to be sold to currently undisclosed buyers, with those contracts pending, while Compulsion and Double Fine will be returned to the management teams of those studios, which will take full control of their IP and revenue streams.

> Arkane Lyon, the studio behind the upcoming Blade game, has entered 'required consultation with its Works Council' to begin looking at 'potential strategic options'. These discussions could take months.

> Ninja Theory has cancelled Project Mara, its experimental photorealistic horror title that was revealed in 2020. Studio Head Dom Matthews explained that Senua, the studio's third title in the series, requires all of the studio's effort.

> Microsoft said none of its first-party publicly announced games or projects are being cancelled as part of the Xbox Reset layoffs.

> Obsidian Entertainment has canceled multiple projects, including a planned sequel to Avowed, and laid off a quarter of its staff as it starts work on a new game in the Fallout series. The new Fallout title will be led by Obsidian studio design director Josh Sawyer, who previously directed Fallout: New Vegas.

> IO Interactive, the studio behind Hitman and 007 First Light, is facing layoffs after the external partner on its online RPG Project Fantasy ended the relationship. A Bloomberg report confirmed Xbox as Project Fantasy's publisher.

> The layoffs come despite the strong launch of 007: First Light, which sold 3 million copies in two weeks and earned broadly positive reviews.

> Hideo Kojima's Xbox console-exclusive horror game, OD, is reportedly safe amidst reports and rumors of studio closures, layoffs, and game cancellations at Xbox.

> Xbox chief strategy officer Matthew Ball said that players can expect a 'reliable pipeline' of exclusives going forward, explaining it was important for Xbox to announce the exclusivity of both Gears of War E-Day and Clockwork Revolution simultaneously 'so that people understood this was not a one-off.' CEO Asha Sharma said the approach will be 'case-by-case.'

**Live-service bifurcation.** The category is splitting into durable hits and fast failures rather than uniformly declining: Embark/Nexon's **Arc Raiders** passed 16M sales as a self-declared "blockbuster" [[arc-raiders-branded-global-blockbuster-after-hitting-14m-sales-6022f364]], while Highguard died in 45 days [[highguard-is-permanently-shutting-down-on-march-12-3599cd1e]], Last Flag shed half its team a month post-launch [[last-flag-dev-night-street-games-lays-off-about-a-dozen-staff-fa98bc05]], and **Bungie ended active Destiny 2 development** (final update June 9) with a WARN-quantified third layoff wave of **292 jobs effective July 9, 2026** — most of the Destiny team and some Marathon devs [[sony-lays-off-most-of-the-destiny-team-and-some-marathon-devs-ce93e0a4]] — and no Destiny 3 greenlit [[bungie-plans-layoffs-after-ending-destiny-2-development-40fe16c3]]. Rec Room's shutdown is being read as a metaverse-bubble signal [[another-sign-that-the-metaverse-winter-is-here-social-vr-platform-rec-room-is-shutting-down-june-1st-2026-583ad629]].

> After just 15 weeks on shelves, Nexon said Arc Raiders defied the standard quick-up-quick-down trajectory of a hit game to establish itself as a blockbuster franchise.

**Standout releases.** The year's commercial leader is a surprise: **Meccha Chameleon**, a **$5.99 two-person indie** hide-and-seek multiplayer game (launched June 10), passed **15 million copies in under a month** to become **2026's fastest- and best-selling game**, outselling the entire AAA slate including Resident Evil Requiem, Crimson Desert, and 007 First Light [[viral-indie-hit-meccha-chameleon-sells-a-massive-15-million-copies-in-less-than-a-month-becomes-the-fastest-and-best-selling-game-of-the-year-21790e00]] — a figure that supersedes the earlier ~3M-in-one-week report and stands as the clearest 2026 data point that a low-budget, low-price viral hit can outrun the year's marquee AAA releases. On critical reception, **Mina the Hollower** (Yacht Club Games) shipped as 2026's highest-rated game (~93 Metacritic) [[round-up-mina-the-hollower-earns-critical-acclaim-best-rated-game-of-2026-in-first-reviews-9e68e0dd]]; **007 First Light** became IO Interactive's best-reviewed and fastest-selling premium game (1.5M units in 24h, 3M in two weeks) [[007-first-light-review-youth-in-revolt-f35b7ab2]] [[007-first-light-has-topped-1-5-million-sales-2978ba61]]; Forza Horizon 6 [[forza-horizon-6-review-the-best-racing-series-is-back-and-better-2679cc0e]], Pokemon Pokopia (the highest-rated Pokemon game) [[pokopia-becomes-highest-rated-pokemon-game-on-metacritic-934cabcc]], and Subnautica 2 (4M+ units in 17 days) [[subnautica-2-hits-four-million-sales-166c6d1e]] round out the slate. The counter-case — and 2026's clearest critical-acclaim-does-not-guarantee-survival postmortem — is **ZA/UM's *Zero Parades: For Dead Spies***, the studio's first game since *Disco Elysium* (2019): it reviewed well (**83 Metacritic, 8/10 GameSpot**) [[zero-parades-for-dead-spies-the-kotaku-review-8ea0dcbb]] [[zero-parades-dev-announces-layoffs-despite-game-s-critical-acclaim-af413b99]], yet two months after its May 2026 launch ZA/UM served redundancy/at-risk notices to **up to 32 staff — nearly a third of the studio** — stating that "although Zero Parades reviewed well, its commercial performance has not enabled us to sustain a studio of our current size" [[za-um-studios-to-lay-off-up-to-32-workers-two-months-after-releasing-zero-parades-for-dead-spies-1b55152f]]. It is the sharpest 2026 instance of a favorably-reviewed high-profile sequel that could not convert acclaim into commercial survival. On the platform-momentum front, Pocketpair's **Palworld** exited early access with its free **1.0** launch (July 10, 40M lifetime players) [[palworld-celebrates-40m-players-since-early-access-as-it-heads-toward-its-july-10-1-0-launch-d703a98d]], and its **855,525 concurrent-player Steam peak** landed it in SteamDB's all-time top 15 (just below Baldur's Gate 3) — making Palworld the **first title ever to hold two separate spots on Steam's all-time top-15 concurrent list** [[palworld-devs-had-high-expectations-for-1-0-but-with-over-855-000-concurrent-steam-players-lead-says-this-is-staggering-f7c778a0]]. The remaster lane produced 2026's clearest back-catalog win: **Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced** (July 9) sold **2 million copies on day one**, peaked at **99,451 concurrent Steam players — an all-time high for the AC series on the platform**, topped Twitch on launch day, and reviewed at **~84 Metacritic, the best-received AC since the 2013 original** — a commercial bright spot for a restructuring Ubisoft and a validation of the lower-risk remaster/back-catalog reinvestment strategy running through 2026 [[assassin-s-creed-black-flag-resynced-launches-with-2-million-copies-sold-10326ffc]].

> Meccha Chameleon sold more than 15 million copies in less than a month, becoming both the fastest-selling and best-selling game of the year so far. It has become the best-selling game of 2026, defeating major AAA titles like Resident Evil Requiem, Crimson Desert, and 007: First Light.

> Palworld has crossed 40 million players, a milestone developer Pocketpair confirmed just days before the game leaves early access. 1.0 is a free update.

> With more than 855,000 concurrent players, Palworld 1.0 has climbed into SteamDB's all-time top 15, sitting just behind Baldur's Gate 3. Palworld now occupies two separate spots on Steam's all-time highest-concurrent-player list: once at rank 3 for its Early Access launch, and again for its 1.0 release.

> Mina the Hollower has earned an overall Metacritic score of 93, and this makes it the highest-rated game of 2026 so far on the website, beating the 91 score for Forza Horizon 6, and the 89 ratings handed to Pokemon Pokopia and Resident Evil Requiem.

> 007 First Light managed to sell 1.5 million units in its first 24 hours, as shared by IO Interactive who both developed and published the game.

> Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced has sold 2 million copies on its first day of availability, following its July 9 release. The game reached a peak of 99,451 concurrent players on Steam within 24 hours of launch, the highest concurrent player count ever recorded for an Assassin's Creed title on the platform, and ranked number one on Twitch on July 9.

> Zero Parades: For Dead Spies is an excellent Disco Elysium successor that still establishes its own identity.

> ZA/UM has served redundancy or at-risk notices to up to 32 staffers at the studio, sharing the news on July 17. Although Zero Parades reviewed well, its commercial performance has not enabled us to sustain a studio of our current size.

> Despite receiving critical acclaim with an 8/10 review from GameSpot and an 83 on Metacritic, the game's commercial performance was insufficient to sustain the studio at its current size.

**Labor, genAI & regulation.** Developer sentiment toward generative AI is majority-negative — the GDC 2026 State of the Industry survey found ~52% view it as harmful and only ~7% positive [[one-third-of-game-workers-using-genai-but-half-think-it-s-bad-dd26fbdb]]; Wizards of the Coast declined to recognize the MTG Arena union (NLRB election June 2, with genAI governance a core demand) [[wizards-of-the-coast-declines-to-recognize-game-worker-union-dac45b87]]. Organized labor is mobilizing on both sides of the Atlantic in response to the 2026 contraction: the CWA held a press conference with multiple Microsoft Xbox unions ahead of the July 6 cuts, demanding advance notice, temporary hiring freezes, and **two years of recall rights** — workers "will not be treated as disposable" [[xbox-union-workers-say-they-will-not-be-treated-as-disposable-6136e5ed]]; and post-July-6 the response escalated from statements to physical protest, with the Bethesda/ZeniMax OneBGS union calling a **'Save Our Devs' march for July 15** across Rockville, Austin, Dallas, and Montreal — counting **440 union positions eliminated** and demanding preferential transfers into open Microsoft roles, stronger severance and extended healthcare, and recall rights [[we-mean-business-xbox-union-workers-fighting-back-with-save-our-devs-rally-on-july-15-80020d3b]] [[bethesda-union-workers-to-protest-mass-layoffs-next-week-eb4a5694]]. The dispute has now escalated from protest marches to **formal NLRB proceedings**: on July 15 the CWA filed **unfair labor practice charges** against Microsoft and numerous Xbox subsidiaries (Xbox, ZeniMax Media, id Software, Bethesda Game Studios), alleging Microsoft **failed to engage in decisional bargaining before announcing the July 6 cuts** — bad-faith bargaining, coercive actions, and unilateral contract changes — with a parallel ULP filed in Canada [[unions-take-legal-action-against-microsoft-for-allegedly-mishandling-xbox-layoffs-3cf8b6e5]]. In France, **Ubisoft Barcelona began a rolling strike (July 1–17)** over 51 layoffs (~28% of the studio, restructured to Rainbow Six only) [[ubisoft-barcelona-to-strike-following-latest-restructuring-which-will-layoff-51-developers-52d572c2]], and striking Quantic Dream developers warn **Star Wars Eclipse "literally cannot be finished"** if a redundancy plan cutting ~115 roles proceeds [[striking-quantic-dream-developers-claim-star-wars-eclipse-is-in-big-trouble-if-the-studio-continues-with-mass-layoffs-c275f675]]. On regulation, the **Stop Killing Games** movement scored its biggest North-American win with California's AB 1921 [[stop-killing-games-bill-passes-california-assembly-1103ca83]], but the **European Commission rejected a binding preservation rule**, committing instead to an industry code of conduct on games' end-of-life — a partial, non-binding outcome the ESA had favored, with organizers vowing "the fight goes on" [[stop-killing-games-says-the-fight-goes-on-after-european-commission-rejects-proposed-rule-changes-e7cd32fb]].

> 36 percent of game industry professionals are using generative AI tools as part of their job, though half of them think the technology is having a negative impact on the industry.

> The conference was opened by the vice president for CWA District 9, Frank Arce, who condemned mass layoffs both past and potential, saying Microsoft workers "will not be treated as disposable."

> Union members announced a 'Save Our Devs' March across all studios on Wednesday July 15th in response to recent mass layoffs at Xbox and its subsidiaries. A total of 440 union positions were eliminated across the Bethesda and ZeniMax unions during the mass layoffs.

> In what is becoming a stressful annual routine, Microsoft has decided to lay off thousands, including MANY of us at Bethesda Games Studios. We lost dozens of programmers, artists, designers and testers. Many of whom worked at BGS for decades.

> Labor unions in the United States and Canada have filed unfair labor practice (ULP) charges against Xbox maker Microsoft for allegedly mishandling its latest round of mass layoffs. The CWA filed the ULP against Microsoft and numerous Xbox subsidiaries on July 15, 2026, for allegedly failing to provide information to labor union CWA and engaging in bad faith bargaining, coercive actions, and unilateral changes to employee contracts.

> Ubisoft Barcelona will go on strike following the studio's latest restructuring, which will see 51 developers laid off — roughly 28% of the studio's pre-downsizing workforce.

> Quantic Dream developers went on strike over layoffs, with one claiming that Star Wars Eclipse's development has been anything but smooth, and the ambitious narrative adventure now faces the potential risk of being canceled altogether.

> The European Commission said it will not propose any new laws requiring that publishers keep their games operational after their servers are shut down, stating that 'at this stage it cannot propose a legal obligation to keep videogames playable after they stop being provided commercially.'

**Platform momentum.** Switch 2 is now a first-class AAA target — Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 returns to Nintendo natively [[call-of-duty-is-finally-returning-to-one-hugely-popular-platform-be402ce2]] and FF7 Rebirth arrived (MC 86) [[final-fantasy-vii-rebirth-available-now-on-switch-2-and-xbox-series-70f3590d]] — while Sony **reversed its six-year PC-port strategy**, making first-party single-player titles permanent PlayStation exclusives [[playstation-boss-says-single-player-games-will-not-come-to-pc-going-forward-52fb26b0]].

> PlayStation Studios CEO Hermen Hulst confirmed in an internal town hall on May 18 that the company's narrative single-player games will remain PlayStation exclusives permanently, with Wolverine explicitly included and no PC version planned.

**Physical media reaches its first hard end-date.** 2026 is the year the platform holders began actively exiting physical media, making disc distribution a distinct current-state dimension rather than a GTA-6 footnote. Sony announced it will **halt production and manufacturing of new physical PlayStation game discs starting January 2028** [[sony-ending-all-physical-playstation-game-discs-starting-in-2028-e3f5c0b9]] — the digital share of full-game unit sales having climbed from just **13% at the 2013 PS4 launch to nearly 80% by 2025** [[sony-ending-all-physical-playstation-game-discs-starting-in-2028-e3f5c0b9]]. Analysts read this as an acceleration point rather than an isolated move: they frame GTA 6 going disc-less as the "starting gun," with Sony's discontinuation landing just **seven days later**, and warn the shift "significantly accelerates" physical media's decline even if discs do not vanish overnight [[we-re-one-step-closer-to-death-of-physical-games-analysts-say-547d5ac8]]. The **2028 hardware generation is expected to match**: analysts say the **PlayStation 6 and Xbox's next console (codename Project Helix) are almost certain to launch with no disc drive at all** [[playstation-6-project-helix-will-almost-certainly-be-digital-only-launch-in-2028-analysts-say-6f1c335b]] — this is analyst forecasting, not a platform-holder confirmation, but it converges with Sony's disc cutoff to point at an all-digital next generation across both major consoles. Read together with the GTA 6 disc standoff below, the current-state picture is that the disc-less shift is **platform-driven and structural**, not a single-publisher choice. **Counter-current (July 15):** Xbox is building a bridge rather than burning it — codenamed **Positron**, entering Insider testing, the program converts eligible Xbox One and Xbox Series X discs into permanent account-based digital licenses at no charge, with the entitlement **tied to the physical disc and transferable with it** (selling or gifting the disc moves the license to the new owner) [[xbox-is-teasing-something-big-for-next-week-amid-disc-to-digital-rumors-6ef919a5]]. Whether Positron meaningfully preserves consumer ownership value or merely smooths the one-way library transition is unresolved, but it is the first platform-holder feature explicitly designed to bridge physical-to-digital ownership in the 2026 disc-less environment.

> PlayStation announced that it will stop producing and manufacturing new physical video game discs starting in January 2028.

> Only 13 percent of total full game unit sales were digital when the PS4 launched in 2013, but by 2025, that share had rocketed to nearly 80 percent.

> Analysts argued that the biggest game in the world going disc-less was less a one-off and more a starting gun. Seven days later, Sony announced its disc discontinuation.

> Analysts say the PlayStation 6 and Xbox's Project Helix are almost certain to launch with no disc drive at all.

**GTA 6 is the dominant anticipated release — and the industry-direction split.** GTA 6 is the year's marquee event, confirmed for **November 19, 2026**, with Rockstar revealing the picture-collage cover art and confirming **pre-orders open June 25, 2026** — eliminating remaining Q4-slip ambiguity [[gta-6-pre-orders-open-next-week-rockstar-reveals-cover-art-39d85d54]]. Pricing is now confirmed: **$79.99 standard / $99.99 Ultimate Edition** (the Ultimate tier bundling special vehicles, weapons, and apparel plus a free month of the GTA+ subscription) [[gta-6-reveals-80-price-tag-and-ultimate-edition-bonuses-648748fe]]. The "physical" launch has become the year's flashpoint for the digital-ownership debate: boxed copies — available November 12, 2026 (a week ahead of release) to support pre-loading — contain only a **digital download code, no disc** [[gta-6-reveals-80-price-tag-and-ultimate-edition-bonuses-648748fe]]. The combination drew immediate consumer backlash and a **-3% Take-Two stock drop** on the pre-order open (a classic sell-the-news reaction; bulls had expected $90–$100 [[gta-6-pre-orders-send-take-two-stock-down-as-price-and-launch-details-disappoint-e1fe4146]]) on both the $80 tier (an industry-leading price point critics fear sets a precedent) and the code-in-a-box format that strips resale and trade-in value [[the-internet-reacts-to-grand-theft-auto-6s-price-and-missing-physical-disc-7d2426d0]], and produced a concrete consequence: two physical-media retailers — **Video Games Plus** (a 30-year North American chain) and **Loot Box Gaming** — publicly **refused to stock GTA 6** at launch unless a disc-based edition ships [[two-game-retailers-are-refusing-to-sell-gta-6-until-theres-a-disc-71975e49]]. That standoff will **not be resolved by a later disc SKU**: The Hollywood Reporter reports there are no plans to print GTA 6 discs at all — not at launch and not months after — with an earlier Rockstar customer-support email that suggested a later disc "turned out to be misleading" [[gta-6-reportedly-won-t-get-disc-copy-even-months-after-launch-eb46ee46]], settling the previously open question of whether the refusing retailers might be placated by a later disc edition. Analysts remain openly divided on what GTA 6 means for the industry: Circana forecasts a $62.8B record year driven by GTA 6's platform-wide lift [[gta-6-could-help-make-2026-gaming-s-biggest-year-ever-analysts-say-b29e7b47]]; van Dreunen (NYU Stern) argues the title will concentrate revenue at the top while structural problems — layoffs, studio closures, mid-tier squeeze — continue unaddressed: "after the high comes the hangover" [[gta-6-won-t-save-the-struggling-games-industry-expert-says-0b364650]]. The contraction running in parallel with record macro-metrics (the $201.6B 2025 high, the Xbox reset, the Ubisoft wind-down, EA cutting Battlefield staff after a record launch) illustrates the van Dreunen case even before GTA 6 ships.

> Rockstar Games has officially revealed the GTA 6 cover art and confirmed that pre-orders for Grand Theft Auto VI start on June 25, 2026. It's slated to release on November 19, 2026.

> GTA VI will start at $80 at launch, with an Ultimate Edition that costs $100. The physical version of Grand Theft Auto VI, containing a download code inside the box, will be available starting November 12, 2026 to support pre-loading.

> On top of the $80 price tag, the game won't feature a physical disc at launch, with boxed copies instead including a digital download code. People have expressed frustration about this approach, with some criticizing that $80 for a game that can't be traded in or sold, and you don't even get a disc, feels unreasonable.

> Two video game retailers have confirmed they won't be selling GTA 6 because it isn't shipping on a physical disc and will instead be digital-only at launch.

> According to The Hollywood Reporter, at this point in time, there are no plans for Grand Theft Auto VI discs to be printed — not at launch, and not months after.

Unresolved disagreements and open threads are tracked in `## Disputes` and `## Open questions` below.

## Recent updates

### Updates 2026-07-25

**Halo: Campaign Evolved reviews: 81 on Metacritic — the lowest-rated Halo ever, despite strong scores, due to PC issues and always-online requirement.** The critical aggregation for *Halo: Campaign Evolved* settled at 81 on Metacritic — bested historically by *Halo 3: ODST* (83) and vastly below *Halo: Combat Evolved*'s 97 [[halo-campaign-evolved-is-tracking-as-the-lowest-rated-halo-yet-aa1fce8c]]. The drag on the aggregate comes not from hostile reviews but from structural concerns: PC version issues and a mandatory always-online requirement with no offline play — a constraint that cuts against the premium single-player framing the game launched with. Notably, this is the first Halo title ever playable on a PlayStation console, making the launch simultaneously the franchise's widest platform reach and its lowest critical benchmark. For the page's ongoing tracking of premium-single-player-vs-live-service economics, this is another data point: even a beloved franchise reissue with strong underlying scores can absorb a meaningful critical penalty when platform/DRM constraints run counter to expectations.

> Halo: Campaign Evolved's current score of 81 is bested by Halo 3: ODST, which has a score of 83, while the original Halo: Combat Evolved had a Metacritic score of 97. [[halo-campaign-evolved-is-tracking-as-the-lowest-rated-halo-yet-aa1fce8c]]

> Despite genuinely strong reviews, this is shaping up to be the lowest-rated Halo ever aggregated, largely due to PC issues and always-online requirements with no offline play. [[halo-campaign-evolved-is-tracking-as-the-lowest-rated-halo-yet-aa1fce8c]]

> This is the first Halo game ever playable on a PlayStation console. [[halo-campaign-evolved-is-tracking-as-the-lowest-rated-halo-yet-aa1fce8c]]

**EA's $55B Saudi PIF-led acquisition gets EU antitrust approval (July 23); Foreign Subsidies Regulation scrutiny still pending July 30.** The European Commission cleared the $55 billion acquisition of Electronic Arts by an investor group including Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund, Jared Kushner's Affinity Partners, and Silver Lake on July 23, 2026, finding it would not raise competition concerns [[saudi-arabia-s-polarising-ea-buyout-approved-under-eu-merger-rules-efddaf75]]. However the EU is separately scrutinizing the deal under its **Foreign Subsidies Regulation**, described as "a bigger hurdle," with a decision due by July 30, 2026 [[saudi-arabia-s-polarising-ea-buyout-approved-under-eu-merger-rules-efddaf75]]. EU antitrust clearance is one of the major gating requirements for the deal to close; the FSR review is the last significant outstanding hurdle. This advances the consolidation arc already tracked on this page: the EA take-private, once completed, would remove one of the four largest Western publishers from the public markets and transfer ownership to sovereign wealth and private equity capital.

> A group of investors including Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund has secured EU antitrust approval for its $55 billion acquisition of video game developer Electronic Arts, the European Commission said on July 23, 2026. [[saudi-arabia-s-polarising-ea-buyout-approved-under-eu-merger-rules-efddaf75]]

> The Commission said the acquisition would not raise competition concerns. The EU executive is also scrutinising the deal under its Foreign Subsidies Regulation, seen as a bigger hurdle, with a decision due by July 30. [[saudi-arabia-s-polarising-ea-buyout-approved-under-eu-merger-rules-efddaf75]]

**Rockstar Games Workers Union requests voluntary recognition — four months before GTA 6 launch.** The Rockstar Games Workers Union (RGWU), backed by the Independent Workers' Union of Great Britain (IWGB), has formally requested voluntary recognition from Rockstar Games [[rockstar-games-employees-request-voluntary-recognition-of-union-34382f6f]]. If Rockstar agrees, the union could bargain legally over pay and working conditions. If Rockstar refuses, the case goes to the UK's Central Arbitration Committee for statutory recognition proceedings [[rockstar-games-employees-request-voluntary-recognition-of-union-34382f6f]]. The timing — four months before GTA 6's November 2026 launch — is notable: crunch concerns at Rockstar have been tracked by the press for years, and the union push arrives at the highest-stakes pre-launch window in gaming history. Prior rockstar labor reporting is on file in [[rockstar-accused-of-ignoring-pay-inequity-and-mandating-crunch-5baa22b1]]. A game studio seeking union recognition while GTA 6 is in final stretch is structurally novel territory; Rockstar's response will likely be a significant labor-beat story through Q4 2026.

> The Rockstar Games Workers Union, backed by the Independent Workers' Union of Great Britain, has requested voluntary recognition from Rockstar Games, four months ahead of the GTA 6 launch. [[rockstar-games-employees-request-voluntary-recognition-of-union-34382f6f]]

> If Rockstar agrees to voluntary recognition, the union would be able to bargain legally over pay and working conditions. If it refuses, the case goes to the UK's Central Arbitration Committee for statutory recognition. [[rockstar-games-employees-request-voluntary-recognition-of-union-34382f6f]]

**Ubisoft Q1 FY2026-27: €255.8M net bookings (-9.2% YoY); AC Black Flag Resynced 3.5M copies in 14 days, 84 on Metacritic.** Ubisoft reported first-quarter FY2026-27 net bookings of €255.8 million, slightly above its target of ~€250M but down 9.2% year-on-year against a high comparison base that included *Assassin's Creed Shadows* [[ubisoft-reports-first-quarter-2026-27-sales-3c15efd2]]. The standout from the quarter: *Assassin's Creed: Black Flag Resynced* achieved 84 on both OpenCritic and Metacritic, with 3.5 million copies sold-in to date — exceeding annual expectations within its first 14 days [[ubisoft-reports-first-quarter-2026-27-sales-3c15efd2]]. (Note: "sold-in" is retail/wholesale shipments, not confirmed consumer sales.) The AC Black Flag Resynced numbers provide important data on Ubisoft's premium-single-player remaster strategy: 3.5M in 14 days on a well-received remake of a beloved 2013 open-world pirate title suggests the back-catalog remaster route has genuine commercial legs for Ubisoft at a time when its new-IP output has been under intense shareholder pressure.

> First-quarter 2026-27 net bookings totaled EUR 255.8 million, slightly above the target of around EUR 250.0 million, but down 9.2% year-on-year against a high comparison base that included Assassin's Creed Shadows. [[ubisoft-reports-first-quarter-2026-27-sales-3c15efd2]]

> Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced achieved 84 on OpenCritic and Metacritic, with 3.5 million copies sold-in to date, exceeding annual expectations within its first 14 days. [[ubisoft-reports-first-quarter-2026-27-sales-3c15efd2]]

**Palworld 1.0 milestone: ~30.5M copies sold, $700M gross software revenue; 1.8M new sales at launch.** Following the July 10 version 1.0 launch, Palworld has reached approximately 30.5 million copies sold and an estimated $700 million in gross software revenue, according to Alinea Analytics estimates [[palworld-1-0-pushes-the-hit-over-30m-copies-sold-18cd9f1f]]. Around 86% of premium sales came from Steam (~27.1M copies); the 1.0 launch with a 30% discount generated approximately 1.8 million additional estimated sales [[palworld-1-0-pushes-the-hit-over-30m-copies-sold-18cd9f1f]]. Palworld has been tracked on this page since its January 2024 early-access explosion; the 1.0 launch converts that viral early-access moment into a full commercial milestone. The 86% Steam concentration and the effectiveness of the launch discount as a conversion mechanism are both meaningful data points for the indie-commercial-strategy thread the page tracks alongside the AAA contraction story.

> Palworld has reportedly sold approximately 30.5 million copies and generated $700 million in gross software revenue following its 1.0 launch, according to estimates from Alinea Analytics. [[palworld-1-0-pushes-the-hit-over-30m-copies-sold-18cd9f1f]]

> Around 86% of the game's premium sales came from Steam, representing approximately 27.1 million copies. Version 1.0 launched on July 10, with a 30% launch discount helping generate another 1.8 million estimated sales. [[palworld-1-0-pushes-the-hit-over-30m-copies-sold-18cd9f1f]]

### Updates 2026-07-24

**The Duskbloods: FromSoftware confirms August 2026 closed Network Test for Switch 2 PvPvE exclusive.** FromSoftware announced that *The Duskbloods* — its Nintendo Switch 2-exclusive PvPvE multiplayer game for up to eight players — will hold a closed Network Test across five sessions from August 21–23, 2026 [[fromsoftware-s-the-duskbloods-confirms-network-test-dates-65b24735]]. Sign-ups opened July 22 and run until July 28, with participants notified August 7. This is significant on three fronts: (1) The Duskbloods is a deliberate departure from FromSoftware's defining single-player Soulsborne formula — a live-service-flavored PvPvE multiplayer title — marking one of the highest-profile Switch 2 exclusives from a prestige studio; (2) the Network Test format (server-load testing for up to 8 simultaneous players) signals that FromSoftware is treating multiplayer infrastructure as a genuine first-class concern; (3) as one of the few high-profile games the page tracks remaining Switch 2-exclusive at a time when many former "exclusives" have gone multiplatform, The Duskbloods' test scope will be the first real signal of its ambitions.

> The Duskbloods is a PvPvE multiplayer game for up to eight players, marking a departure from FromSoftware's typically single-player-focused Soulsborne titles, and is one of the highest-profile Switch 2 exclusives on the horizon. [[fromsoftware-s-the-duskbloods-confirms-network-test-dates-65b24735]]

> The Network Test will run across five sessions between August 21 and August 23, 2026. Groups of eight players will join a single session while FromSoftware tests game balance, multiplayer and server load. [[fromsoftware-s-the-duskbloods-confirms-network-test-dates-65b24735]]

**Games industry M&A hits $2.3bn in Q2 2026 — "post-pandemic" deal-making high, but stocks still falling.** Games industry M&A reached $2.3 billion across 54 transactions in Q2 2026, a level analysts describe as matching the "Covid-era boom" [[games-industry-m-a-reaches-2-3bn-in-q2-2026-e36c8115]]. The largest deal was Scopely's $1 billion acquisition of Loom Games (mid-market content acquisition); WeMade's founder stake sale to NeoPulse (~$591m) was the largest disclosed deal by value. The M&A recovery quantifies a structural thread the page has tracked all year: while publisher-level deals (EA $55B take-private, Ubisoft Vantage Studios, Embracer spin-off) drew most coverage, the mid-market layer ($100M+ content acquisitions) is where deal volume is accelerating. The counter-signal: gaming stocks are NOT recovering in parallel — mobile-first Western publishers fell 13% YTD, mobile-first Asian publishers 37–42%, suggesting M&A appetite reflects strategic repositioning rather than market confidence.

> Games industry mergers and acquisitions reached US$2.3 billion in Q2 2026 across 54 transactions, with dealmaking now at a 'post-pandemic' high matching the Covid-era boom. [[games-industry-m-a-reaches-2-3bn-in-q2-2026-e36c8115]]

> Despite the M&A recovery, gaming stocks continued a broader decline, with mobile-first Western publishers dropping 13% year-to-date and mobile-first Asian publishers tumbling between 37% and 42%. [[games-industry-m-a-reaches-2-3bn-in-q2-2026-e36c8115]]

**Vermila Studios: entire 19-person staff laid off, closure possible — another indie postmortem of good reviews ≠ commercial survival.** Vermila Studios, developer of Blumhouse-published survival horror *Crisol: Theater of Idols* (February 2026), has laid off all 19 employees, with CEO David Carrasco warning that a full studio closure is "a feasible possibility in the upcoming months" [[vermila-studios-lays-off-staff-studio-closure-a-feasible-possibility-in-coming-months-eea51a99]]. Crisol launched to generally positive reviews but could not sustain commercial operations five months post-launch. This adds another data point to 2026's clearest recurring postmortem pattern — alongside ZA/UM (Zero Parades) and La Moustache (Last Flag) — where critical reception could not translate into commercial survival: a Blumhouse-branded horror game, in one of horror's strongest years, closed its developer within months of launch [[vermila-studios-lays-off-staff-studio-closure-a-feasible-possibility-in-coming-months-eea51a99]].

> Vermila Studios, the developer behind Blumhouse-published survival horror game Crisol: Theater of Idols, has laid off its entire staff, with CEO David Carrasco confirming 19 employees have been let go and warning that a full studio closure is 'a feasible possibility in the upcoming months.' [[vermila-studios-lays-off-staff-studio-closure-a-feasible-possibility-in-coming-months-eea51a99]]

> Crisol: Theater of Idols launched in February 2026 for PS5, PC and Xbox Series X/S to generally positive reviews, but the studio could not sustain operations five months after release. [[vermila-studios-lays-off-staff-studio-closure-a-feasible-possibility-in-coming-months-eea51a99]]

### Updates 2026-07-23

**Xbox Backward Compatibility on PC launches (July 22): four classic OG Xbox titles, cross-buy license, handhelds included.** Xbox announced and launched Backward Compatibility on PC on July 22, 2026 — the first wave brings four original Xbox titles: *BLiNX: The Time Sweeper*, *Conker: Live and Reloaded*, *Crimson Skies: High Road to Revenge*, and *Fuzion Frenzy* [[play-more-of-the-games-you-love-wherever-you-play-with-xbox-backward-compatibility-on-pc-c2c50831]]. The four titles are available for purchase on PC and included with all Xbox Game Pass plans. Critically, existing digital console licenses for these titles carry over to PC: "if you already own a digital license for these games on console, that license can carry over to your PC or handheld, meaning you only need to purchase the game once" [[play-more-of-the-games-you-love-wherever-you-play-with-xbox-backward-compatibility-on-pc-c2c50831]]. Xbox VP of next generation Jason Ronald framed this as "the beginning of a broader effort to preserve Xbox games from the past and bring them to PC over time." The initiative supports the ROG Xbox Ally and Xbox Ally X handhelds. This connects to several threads on this page: (1) the cross-buy/digital-license angle is a modest positive data point against the game-ownership-erosion narrative (Stop Killing Games, Sony ending physical discs [[sony-ending-all-physical-playstation-game-discs-starting-in-2028-e3f5c0b9]]); (2) Xbox's PC preservation push is a counter-current to the Xbox reset layoffs, adding content investment signals alongside the studio-closure wave; (3) the handheld focus (ROG Xbox Ally) extends the series-X console library to portable form, which Sony has not yet publicly committed to for PS6.

> Xbox Backward Compatibility on PC makes it easier to play classic Xbox games from the past across PC and handhelds, including the ROG Xbox Ally and Xbox Ally X. [[play-more-of-the-games-you-love-wherever-you-play-with-xbox-backward-compatibility-on-pc-c2c50831]]

> If you already own a digital license for these games on console, that license can carry over to your PC or handheld, meaning you only need to purchase the game once. [[play-more-of-the-games-you-love-wherever-you-play-with-xbox-backward-compatibility-on-pc-c2c50831]]

**Roblox Grow a Garden: 21.96 million concurrent users on July 19, new all-time gaming concurrent record.** On July 19, Grow a Garden peaked at **21,963,800 concurrent users** on Roblox — the new all-time record for any gaming platform or experience, surpassing both its own prior record of 21.3 million (June 21 Summer Update) and Fortnite's previous all-time gaming record of 15.3 million concurrent players [[roblox-s-grow-a-garden-had-nearly-22-million-concurrent-users-in-july-5808945e]]. The record also pushed Roblox itself to its highest platform-wide concurrent user count ever. Grow a Garden is a free-to-play farming simulator released March 25, 2026, originally created by a 16-year-old anonymous user; it became the top experience by spending within one month of release. The concurrent-user record is significant for the gaming-industry narrative this page tracks for two reasons: (1) it confirms the ultra-viral, single-developer, zero-marketing indie model (the Meccha Chameleon thread [[viral-indie-hit-meccha-chameleon-sells-a-massive-15-million-copies-in-less-than-a-month-becomes-the-fastest-and-best-selling-game-of-the-year-21790e00]]) as a platform-scale phenomenon, not just a sales-chart curiosity; (2) it sets a concurrent-player ceiling that dwarfs anything AAA studios can claim, in a free platform game with minimal production budget.

> Roblox's record-breaking game Grow a Garden attracted 22 million concurrent users in July, surpassing Fortnite's existing record of 15.3 million concurrent players. [[roblox-s-grow-a-garden-had-nearly-22-million-concurrent-users-in-july-5808945e]]

> The free-to-play farming simulator was released on March 25 and was originally created by a 16-year-old anonymous user. It became the top experience based on spending within one month of release. [[roblox-s-grow-a-garden-had-nearly-22-million-concurrent-users-in-july-5808945e]]

### Updates 2026-07-22

**Laid-off Xbox devs condemn Microsoft: "Good work is not going to save your job."** Game Developer compiled first-person accounts from laid-off Xbox/ZeniMax developers painting a picture of a company where merit is no longer a shield against layoffs [[good-work-is-not-going-to-save-your-job-at-this-company-laid-off-xbox-devs-condemn-microsoft-5e50dfeb]]. Key disclosures: **around 90 percent of the id Software design team** responsible for overseeing AI and gameplay was eliminated, a cut proportionally larger than the 136-headcount WARN Act figure implied [[update-136-workers-have-been-laid-off-at-id-software-98ba024d]]; sources said Xbox "failed to handle the internal layoff announcement with compassion"; and a former long-timer called what is happening to ZeniMax Online Studios a "slow, tortuous death," contrasting the current climate with the ZeniMax of years past where employees expected to retire. The accounts confirm that the Xbox reset is hollowing out institutional knowledge at formerly flagship studios faster than headcount numbers alone convey.

> Around 90 percent of the design team responsible for overseeing AI and gameplay within id Software have been wiped out, according to sources. [[good-work-is-not-going-to-save-your-job-at-this-company-laid-off-xbox-devs-condemn-microsoft-5e50dfeb]]

> Good work is not going to save your job at this company. It used to be, back in the day, that you would come to ZeniMax to retire. [[good-work-is-not-going-to-save-your-job-at-this-company-laid-off-xbox-devs-condemn-microsoft-5e50dfeb]]

### Updates 2026-07-21

**id Software: two-thirds of remaining Doom (2016) devs laid off in the latest Xbox cuts.** Around two-thirds of those remaining at id Software who worked on *Doom (2016)* were laid off at the beginning of July [[two-thirds-of-remaining-doom-2016-devs-laid-off-during-latest-round-of-xbox-cuts-d7c0076d]]. The headline adds qualitative texture to the WARN Act headcount already on record (136 cut per [[update-136-workers-have-been-laid-off-at-id-software-98ba024d]]): this was not a back-office trim but a removal of institutional creative memory on a specific title. Studio head Hugo Martin publicly characterized the impact as minimal; sources told Game Developer that Martin "willfully ignored how losing so much talent and experience would impact the studio" [[two-thirds-of-remaining-doom-2016-devs-laid-off-during-latest-round-of-xbox-cuts-d7c0076d]]. Dispute filed below.

> Around two thirds of those left at id Software who worked on Doom (2016) were laid off at the beginning of the month, according to sources.

> Studio head Hugo Martin suggested the impact was minimal, but sources felt Martin willfully ignored how losing so much talent and experience would impact the studio.

**Todd Howard confirms Bethesda-Obsidian Fallout collaboration timing is right.** Todd Howard told Bloomberg the timing is now right for a collaboration with sister studio Obsidian Entertainment on a new Fallout game — the first since Obsidian's *Fallout: New Vegas* in 2010 [[todd-howard-says-the-timing-is-right-for-a-collaboration-with-obsidian-f1fb056b]]. Howard cited "a huge amount of mutual respect" and said both sides had been looking for the right opportunity ever since. This follows the July 16 report that Obsidian cancelled its planned Avowed sequel to begin work on a new Fallout title [[report-obsidian-cancels-avowed-sequel-to-begin-work-on-a-new-fallout-game-2d7ec4f3]], providing the first direct confirmation from Bethesda's side that the collaboration is actually happening. Open question filed below.

> Todd Howard told Bloomberg that the timing is right for the collaboration on a new Fallout game with sister studio Obsidian Entertainment.

> Howard said there is a huge amount of mutual respect between them, and that both sides had been looking for the right opportunity to collaborate again since 2010's Fallout: New Vegas.

**Netflix: critically-panned FIFA World Cup cloud game was a "top tier" commercial debut despite press reviews.** Netflix disclosed that its FIFA World Cup title and *Unhinged* were "our two most successful cloud game debuts" by engagement metrics — "really solid numbers that put it in the top tier of game performance for us" — despite the FIFA title being branded a "massive missed opportunity" by FourFourTwo and a "modern hellscape" by Aftermath [[netflix-says-its-critically-panned-fifa-world-cup-video-game-was-a-top-tier-performer-78aca96b]]. A concrete data point for the divergence between press critical reception and subscription-platform engagement: in a cloud-gaming context, the addressable audience is large enough that brand recognition and casual curiosity can drive top-tier numbers even when enthusiast press consensus is negative. Open question filed below.

> FIFA and Unhinged became our two most successful cloud game debuts, really solid numbers that put it in the top tier of game performance for us.

> FIFA appears to have succeeded in Netflix's estimations despite being branded a massive missed opportunity by FourFourTwo and a modern hellscape by Aftermath.

### Updates 2026-07-20

**Bethesda doubles down on Starfield amid the ZeniMax reset — a counter-current to the studio's own layoff wave.** On July 17 Bethesda publicly reaffirmed long-term support for *Starfield*, stating the game "remains an important part of our future" and pledging **new Starborn content in 2027** alongside continued stories, gameplay improvements, and investment in fan Creations [[starfield-remains-important-to-bethesda-new-content-coming-in-2027-96ffa43d]]. The studio put concrete engagement numbers behind the commitment: **over 17 million players and nearly a billion hours played**, with more than 40% of players having customized the game via Creations [[starfield-remains-important-to-bethesda-new-content-coming-in-2027-96ffa43d]]. The notable framing here is timing: the roadmap lands in the middle of the Xbox reset that gutted the same corporate parent — id Software (~95 cut), Bethesda Game Studios (35 cut), and a "seemingly gutted" ZeniMax Online — making this a rare piece of counter-evidence to the 2026 "Bethesda/ZeniMax in retreat" narrative rather than another contraction data point. Whether a reduced-headcount division can actually ship the promised 2027 roadmap is the open question (filed below).

> Starfield remains an important part of our future. Bethesda will continue expanding the Settled Systems with new stories, targeted gameplay improvements, and additional updates, with new Starborn content coming to the game in 2027.

> The game has reached over 17 million players who have spent nearly a billion hours playing Starfield. More than 40 percent of Starfield players have customized the game through fan-created Creations.

### Updates 2026-07-19

**ZA/UM lays off up to 32 — critical-acclaim-vs-commercial-failure postmortem for Zero Parades.** Two months after releasing *Zero Parades: For Dead Spies* (May 2026), ZA/UM Studios announced on July 17 that up to **32 staffers have received redundancy or at-risk notices**, citing commercial underperformance despite favorable reviews [[za-um-studios-to-lay-off-up-to-32-workers-two-months-after-releasing-zero-parades-for-dead-spies-1b55152f]]. The studio's statement: **"Although Zero Parades reviewed well, its commercial performance has not enabled us to sustain a studio of our current size"** [[za-um-studios-to-lay-off-up-to-32-workers-two-months-after-releasing-zero-parades-for-dead-spies-1b55152f]]. The game scored **83 on Metacritic and 8/10 from GameSpot** [[zero-parades-dev-announces-layoffs-despite-game-s-critical-acclaim-af413b99]] but was ZA/UM's only second game after the beloved *Disco Elysium* (2019). The cuts reduce a studio of roughly 100 employees (at peak post-unionization in October 2025) by nearly a third; the ZA/UM Workers' Alliance, the recognized union, has been consulted throughout. This is the clearest 2026 case study of the critical-acclaim-does-not-guarantee-survival gap: a high-profile sequel to a 2019 Game of the Year nominee, reviewed at 83, could not sustain its headcount within two months of launch.

> ZA/UM has served redundancy or at-risk notices to up to 32 staffers at the studio, sharing the news on July 17. Although Zero Parades reviewed well, its commercial performance has not enabled us to sustain a studio of our current size. [[za-um-studios-to-lay-off-up-to-32-workers-two-months-after-releasing-zero-parades-for-dead-spies-1b55152f]]

> Despite receiving critical acclaim with an 8/10 review from GameSpot and an 83 on Metacritic, the game's commercial performance was insufficient to sustain the studio at its current size. [[zero-parades-dev-announces-layoffs-despite-game-s-critical-acclaim-af413b99]]

**ZeniMax Online Studios leadership stripped — ESO lost 60%+ of staff in past year.** A Maryland WARN Act notice detailed July 19 reveals the full ZeniMax scope from the July 6 Xbox reset: **213 from ZeniMax Online Studios + 166 from ZeniMax Media = 379 total**, with most notified July 6 and cuts effective September 4, 2026 [[zenimax-online-studios-leadership-is-part-of-the-379-laid-off-workers-9588dc4d]]. The leadership impact is severe: **Joe Burba** (14-year veteran, studio head since July 2025), **ESO executive producer Susan Kath**, production director **Ala Diaz**, and studio game director **Rich Lambert** are all among those laid off [[zenimax-online-studios-leadership-is-part-of-the-379-laid-off-workers-9588dc4d]]. The cumulative damage to Elder Scrolls Online's team is staggering: **ESO has lost at least 60% of its staff over the past year** across multiple waves [[zenimax-online-studios-leadership-is-part-of-the-379-laid-off-workers-9588dc4d]]. This is additive to the "gutted" framing in the July 6 update and establishes a near-complete leadership vacuum at the studio.

> The cuts include Joe Burba, a 14-year veteran of the studio who was appointed to run it last July, as well as The Elder Scrolls Online executive producer Susan Kath, production director Ala Diaz, and studio game director Rich Lambert. Over half of the layoffs come from the Elder Scrolls Online studio, which has lost at least 60 percent of its staff in the past year. [[zenimax-online-studios-leadership-is-part-of-the-379-laid-off-workers-9588dc4d]]

**CWA files ULP charges against Microsoft and Xbox subsidiaries — decisional-bargaining failure.** On July 15, 2026, the Communications Workers of America filed **unfair labor practice charges** against Microsoft and numerous Xbox subsidiaries — including Xbox, ZeniMax Media, id Software, and Bethesda Game Studios — for allegedly **failing to engage in decisional bargaining before announcing the July 6 mass layoffs**, constituting bad faith bargaining, coercive actions, and unilateral contract changes [[unions-take-legal-action-against-microsoft-for-allegedly-mishandling-xbox-layoffs-3cf8b6e5]]. A parallel ULP was filed in Canada. The CWA contends Microsoft did not consult the union before informing union members they had been laid off [[unions-take-legal-action-against-microsoft-for-allegedly-mishandling-xbox-layoffs-3cf8b6e5]]. This escalates the Xbox reset story from protest marches (July 13–16 entries) to formal NLRB proceedings — a legal track that could produce back pay orders, reinstatement rights, or at minimum NLRB-documented findings on Microsoft's bargaining conduct.

> Labor unions in the United States and Canada have filed unfair labor practice (ULP) charges against Xbox maker Microsoft for allegedly mishandling its latest round of mass layoffs. The CWA filed the ULP against Microsoft and numerous Xbox subsidiaries on July 15, 2026, for allegedly failing to provide information to labor union CWA and engaging in bad faith bargaining, coercive actions, and unilateral changes to employee contracts. [[unions-take-legal-action-against-microsoft-for-allegedly-mishandling-xbox-layoffs-3cf8b6e5]]

### Updates 2026-07-17

**Build a Rocket Boy labor protest and the new visibility of game-industry layoffs.** Two sources converge on a structural shift in how game-industry layoffs are being contested publicly. First, IWGB protesters picketed outside Build a Rocket Boy's Leith, Scotland headquarters on July 17, 2026 while the studio held an all-expenses-paid fan playtest — a direct juxtaposition the union called "a kick in the teeth for the fired workers" [[a-kick-in-the-teeth-game-workers-protest-alleged-misconduct-at-mindseye-developer-build-a-rocket-boy-1423e0ae]]. The IWGB estimates more than 400 employees have been laid off at the studio, which began a redundancy process less than two weeks after MindsEye's launch, with further rounds in March and May 2026. The protest positions public-facing fan events against laid-off workers as the new union pressure lever.

> This extravagant playtest event is a waste of money, and a kick in the teeth for the fired workers who are seeing fans brought in to do jobs that would otherwise have been theirs. [[a-kick-in-the-teeth-game-workers-protest-alleged-misconduct-at-mindseye-developer-build-a-rocket-boy-1423e0ae]]

> Former employees protested outside the office of MindsEye studio Build a Rocket Boy as the company held an all-expenses-paid playtest for fans, with the Independent Workers Union of Great Britain (IWGB) protesting at the studio's headquarters in Leith, Scotland. [[a-kick-in-the-teeth-game-workers-protest-alleged-misconduct-at-mindseye-developer-build-a-rocket-boy-1423e0ae]]

Second, Kotaku's labor analysis places this alongside ZeniMax/Bethesda employee rallies protesting the Xbox reset — arguing that coordinated public action (marches, pickets, union organizing) has fundamentally changed the visibility of gaming layoffs [[video-game-companies-can-no-longer-do-layoffs-quietly-13752cf8]]. The ZeniMax and Bethesda rallies drew participants across Texas, Montreal, and California following the cut of 1,600 Xbox employees (with 1,600 more forecast), and the article frames this as a structural change: studios can no longer conduct mass layoffs without public labor resistance.

> ZeniMax and Bethesda employees held rallies to protest Xbox's latest round of layoffs that cost 1,600 people their jobs, with 1,600 more layoffs to come in the future. [[video-game-companies-can-no-longer-do-layoffs-quietly-13752cf8]]

**This is additive context to the Save Our Devs thread already on this page (July 15–16 entries)**: the Build a Rocket Boy protest is a parallel vector — smaller studio, post-launch collapse context, IWGB rather than OneBGS/CWA — demonstrating that the coordinated-public-protest pattern is generalizing beyond Microsoft's union footprint.

### Updates 2026-07-16

**Denshattack! lands as a mechanically-novel indie standout (MC 88, OpenCritic 'Mighty' 87).** Undercoders' train-flipping platformer launched July 15, 2026 day-one on Game Pass Ultimate and PC Game Pass, reviewing strongly across all platforms [[roundup-rave-reviews-for-denshattack-make-it-a-must-play-on-xbox-game-pass-d1ceb894]]. The design hook is a new genre hybrid — ollie, grind and kickflip a customizable train through a domed dystopian Japan — with direction by David Jaumandreu (Ninja Gaiden: Ragebound) and a soundtrack by Tee Lopes (Sonic Mania). At 88 it sits below 2026's reception leaders (Mina the Hollower ~93, Forza Horizon 6 ~91), and its day-one Game Pass placement clouds direct sales visibility, but the 'trainformer' formula and Undercoders/Jaumandreu pedigree make it one of the mid-year indie slate's more distinctive design swings.

> Denshattack! is rated 'Mighty' after being reviewed by 53 critics on OpenCritic, with an overall average score of 87, and is ranked in the top 2% of games and recommended by 94% of critics. [[roundup-rave-reviews-for-denshattack-make-it-a-must-play-on-xbox-game-pass-d1ceb894]]

**'Save Our Devs' rally materializes — first coordinated multi-studio labor action in gaming history.** The OneBGS-organized march that was planned on July 14 went ahead on July 15: hundreds of developers marched simultaneously at six Xbox locations across four cities, protesting Microsoft's July 6 cut of ~3,200 jobs (440 union-represented positions) [[game-workers-march-at-six-xbox-studios-over-440-union-cuts-and-stalled-contracts-ad076cea]]. Marches hit ZeniMax offices in Rockville, Austin, Dallas and Montreal, plus a solidarity rally at Obsidian in Irvine (drawing Blizzard employees) and a concurrent UVW-CWA march at Microsoft's Redmond campus. Unions are leveraging Microsoft's legal obligation to conduct Effects Bargaining, pressing for preferential transfers, stronger severance/extended healthcare, and recall rights.

> The Xbox union layoffs sparked the first coordinated multi-studio labor action in gaming history on July 15, 2026, as hundreds of developers marched simultaneously at six Xbox locations across four cities. [[game-workers-march-at-six-xbox-studios-over-440-union-cuts-and-stalled-contracts-ad076cea]]

### Updates 2026-07-15

**Xbox counters the disc-less shift with "Positron" — a disc-to-digital conversion program entering Insider testing.** Amid a "worth the wait" tease from program lead Brad Rossetti, Xbox is reportedly about to debut **Positron**, a disc-to-digital program that lets owners convert eligible **Xbox One and Xbox Series X** game discs into permanent, account-based **digital licenses at no charge** [[xbox-is-teasing-something-big-for-next-week-amid-disc-to-digital-rumors-6ef919a5]]. Crucially, the entitlement is **tied to the disc itself and transfers with it** — selling or gifting the disc moves the digital license to the new owner's Microsoft Account — and converted **Play Anywhere** titles could also become available on PC and compatible handhelds; the feature debuts first to Xbox Insiders with disc-equipped consoles [[xbox-is-teasing-something-big-for-next-week-amid-disc-to-digital-rumors-6ef919a5]]. This is a distinct current-state development in the physical-media thread: where Sony is ending disc manufacturing (Jan 2028) and GTA 6 ships disc-less, Xbox is building a bridge that preserves a limited form of resale/transfer even as it moves libraries digital.

> The tease is widely rumored to be the first look at Xbox's disc-to-digital program, codenamed Positron, which would let owners convert eligible Xbox One and Xbox Series X game discs into permanent account-based digital licenses without a fee. The entitlement is tied to the disc itself, so if a player sells or gifts the disc, the digital entitlement transfers with it. [[xbox-is-teasing-something-big-for-next-week-amid-disc-to-digital-rumors-6ef919a5]]

**Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced's record launch is shadowed by Ubisoft Barcelona layoffs — a fresh commercial-success-vs-labor disconnect.** Two days after Black Flag Resynced posted an AC-record Steam launch (2M day-one sales, 99,451 concurrent peak), Kotaku reported that the **Ubisoft Barcelona** developers behind the remake are **facing layoffs at the end of the month** after not being assigned to a new project — one affected dev writing "this is not how I imagined it would end" [[assassin-s-creed-devs-celebrate-black-flag-remake-launch-with-layoffs-this-is-not-how-i-imagined-it-would-end-45fb3f59]]. This is a concrete new instance of the pattern (also seen with EA's Battlefield staff) in which a record commercial launch does not protect the developers who shipped it, and connects to the ongoing Ubisoft Barcelona strike over the same restructuring.

> Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced is out today and is already having one of the best Steam launches in the franchise's history. Unfortunately, the celebrations are bittersweet for developers who worked on the game at Ubisoft Barcelona, as the studio's team there is facing layoffs at the end of the month after not being assigned to a new project. [[assassin-s-creed-devs-celebrate-black-flag-remake-launch-with-layoffs-this-is-not-how-i-imagined-it-would-end-45fb3f59]]

**Xbox's own Player Voice feedback portal is being turned against the layoffs.** A post calling to "end studio closures and stop the cycle of layoffs" has become the **top-trending item on Xbox's official Player Voice portal**, gathering **over 3,000 upvotes**, and calling the layoff of 3,200 Xbox workers "unacceptable" [[a-call-for-xbox-to-end-cycle-of-layoffs-is-trending-on-fan-portal-190b54e8]]. Its demands — no layoffs for two years, good-faith union negotiation, no executive bonuses during layoffs, and investing in future developers "instead of AI technologies" — mirror the CWA/OneBGS union asks and extend the Xbox Reset's labor backlash from workers and unions to the broader player base [[a-call-for-xbox-to-end-cycle-of-layoffs-is-trending-on-fan-portal-190b54e8]].

> A call to 'end studio closures and stop the cycle of layoffs' has become the top-trending post on the Xbox Player Voice portal with over 3,000 upvotes, stating the layoff of 3,200 workers at XBOX is unacceptable and demanding no layoffs for 2 years, good-faith union negotiation, and investing in future developers instead of AI technologies. [[a-call-for-xbox-to-end-cycle-of-layoffs-is-trending-on-fan-portal-190b54e8]]

**A DayZ-style survival game pivots from PvPvE to pure co-op PvE after alpha feedback — a small live-service-design signal.** Quantum Quirks' open-world zombie sim **Zeverland** shifted from a **pure PvPvE MMO into a buy-to-play, survival-crafting, co-op PvE game** after its closed alpha, with the studio saying players wanted an entirely cooperative experience [[this-promising-dayz-rival-is-thankfully-pivoting-to-pve-before-launch-625ae731]]. It is a minor case, but a pointed one for the live-service-bifurcation thread: a pre-launch retreat from forced PvP toward buy-to-play co-op, cutting against the saturated PvP-survival field.

> The studio decided to shift Zeverland from a pure MMO into a buy-to-play, survival-crafting, co-op PvE game and confirmed that Zeverland is doubling down on cooperative PvE. [[this-promising-dayz-rival-is-thankfully-pivoting-to-pve-before-launch-625ae731]]

### Updates 2026-07-14

**Bethesda/ZeniMax union stages a 'Save Our Devs' rally on July 15 — the Xbox Reset labor response escalates from statements to physical protest.** Following the July 6 cuts, the OneBGS union announced a **'Save Our Devs' march on Wednesday July 15** at ZeniMax studio offices in **Rockville, Austin, Dallas, and Montreal**, counting **440 union positions eliminated** across the Bethesda and ZeniMax unions [[we-mean-business-xbox-union-workers-fighting-back-with-save-our-devs-rally-on-july-15-80020d3b]]. The union is demanding **preferential transfers** (placing affected BGS workers into open Microsoft roles first), **stronger severance and extended healthcare**, and **recall rights** (laid-off members rehired first when BGS expands) [[we-mean-business-xbox-union-workers-fighting-back-with-save-our-devs-rally-on-july-15-80020d3b]]. The Bethesda union framed the cuts as a recurring pattern, noting that management deemed 10k+ prior cuts 'insufficient' and that BGS lost 'dozens of programmers, artists, designers and testers' with decades of tenure [[bethesda-union-workers-to-protest-mass-layoffs-next-week-eb4a5694]].

> Union members announced a 'Save Our Devs' March across all studios on Wednesday July 15th in response to recent mass layoffs at Xbox and its subsidiaries. A total of 440 union positions were eliminated across the Bethesda and ZeniMax unions during the mass layoffs. The company wants us to accept this as a done deal and quietly disappear. We won't let that happen. [[we-mean-business-xbox-union-workers-fighting-back-with-save-our-devs-rally-on-july-15-80020d3b]]

> In what is becoming a stressful annual routine, Microsoft has decided to lay off thousands, including MANY of us at Bethesda Games Studios. We lost dozens of programmers, artists, designers and testers. Many of whom worked at BGS for decades. [[bethesda-union-workers-to-protest-mass-layoffs-next-week-eb4a5694]]

**Palworld 1.0 sets a Steam concurrency record — first game ever with two entries in Steam's all-time top-15 concurrent peaks.** Palworld 1.0 climbed to a **855,525 concurrent-player peak on Steam** — its **second-highest peak ever** (behind only its January 2024 Early Access debut of ~2.1M) and placing it in **SteamDB's all-time top 15, just below Baldur's Gate 3 (875,343)** [[palworld-devs-had-high-expectations-for-1-0-but-with-over-855-000-concurrent-steam-players-lead-says-this-is-staggering-f7c778a0]]. This makes Palworld the first title to hold **two separate spots on Steam's all-time top-15 concurrent list** [[palworld-devs-had-high-expectations-for-1-0-but-with-over-855-000-concurrent-steam-players-lead-says-this-is-staggering-f7c778a0]].

> With more than 855,000 concurrent players, Palworld 1.0 has climbed into SteamDB's all-time top 15, sitting just behind Baldur's Gate 3. Palworld now occupies two separate spots on Steam's all-time highest-concurrent-player list: once at rank 3 for its Early Access launch, and again for its 1.0 release. [[palworld-devs-had-high-expectations-for-1-0-but-with-over-855-000-concurrent-steam-players-lead-says-this-is-staggering-f7c778a0]]

**Meccha Chameleon is now 2026's best-selling game — a $5.99 two-person indie outselling the year's AAA slate.** The viral hide-and-seek multiplayer game passed **15 million copies in under a month** (launched June 10), becoming the **fastest- and best-selling game of 2026**, ahead of AAA titles including **Resident Evil Requiem, Crimson Desert, and 007: First Light** [[viral-indie-hit-meccha-chameleon-sells-a-massive-15-million-copies-in-less-than-a-month-becomes-the-fastest-and-best-selling-game-of-the-year-21790e00]]. This supersedes the wiki's earlier ~3M-in-one-week figure.

> Meccha Chameleon sold more than 15 million copies in less than a month, becoming both the fastest-selling and best-selling game of the year so far. It has become the best-selling game of 2026, defeating major AAA titles like Resident Evil Requiem, Crimson Desert, and 007: First Light. [[viral-indie-hit-meccha-chameleon-sells-a-massive-15-million-copies-in-less-than-a-month-becomes-the-fastest-and-best-selling-game-of-the-year-21790e00]]

### Updates 2026-07-13

**Obsidian's post-Reset outcome is now concrete: Avowed sequel cancelled, studio pivots to a new Josh Sawyer-led Fallout game.** Bloomberg's Jason Schreier reported (July 8) that Obsidian Entertainment — which lost ~60–70 roles, roughly a quarter of its staff, in the July 6 Xbox Reset — has cancelled multiple projects, including a planned Avowed sequel, and started work on a **new game in the Fallout series led by studio design director Josh Sawyer**, who directed Fallout: New Vegas [[report-obsidian-cancels-avowed-sequel-to-begin-work-on-a-new-fallout-game-2d7ec4f3]]. Per the report, the cancelled Avowed sequel was on track to be announced within the next year but did not fit Xbox CEO Asha Sharma's strategy; a California WARN notice confirms 52 Obsidian layoffs, Bethesda will reportedly co-develop the Fallout title, and Sawyer's team had already been building a Fallout-adjacent RPG, making the pivot a natural fit [[report-obsidian-cancels-avowed-sequel-to-begin-work-on-a-new-fallout-game-2d7ec4f3]]. This is the first *named* project cancellation surfaced under the Reset and sits in tension with Microsoft's official line that "none of our first-party publicly announced games or projects are being cancelled" — technically consistent, since Avowed 2 was never announced, but a concrete cancellation nonetheless.
